   77|       |    /// The returned acknowledgement means Redis accepted the command. It does
   78|       |    /// not guarantee that the record was fsynced or consumed. A connection loss

   99|       |    /// admission then covers wire encoding and connection setup. A missing or

  183|       |    /// Creates or reuses a consumer group and returns its async receiver.
  184|       |    ///
  185|       |    /// A group's cursor is created only when Redis has not seen the group
  186|       |    /// before. For an existing group, `New` or the explicit `resume` policy
  187|       |    /// retains its cursor; an explicit position otherwise returns an error.
  188|       |    /// Unsettled entries remain in the pending entries list when a read future
  189|       |    /// is cancelled or a receiver is closed.
  190|       |    ///
  191|       |    /// # Type Parameters
  192|       |    ///
  193|       |    /// - `'a`: Lifetime shared by the bus borrow and the returned future.
  194|       |    /// # Parameters
  195|       |    ///
  196|       |    /// - `request`: Topic, group, start position, and identity for the
  197|       |    ///   receiver.
  198|       |    ///
  199|       |    ///
  200|       |    /// # Returns
  201|       |    ///
  202|       |    /// A boxed async subscription bound to the requested group.
  203|       |    ///
  204|       |    /// # Errors
  205|       |    ///
  206|       |    /// Returns an SPI error if subscription options are invalid, an existing
  207|       |    /// group's cursor conflicts with an explicit start position, or Redis
  208|       |    /// cannot open a connection or create the requested consumer group.
  209|       |    /// Only the RESP `+OK` reply confirms `XGROUP CREATE`; malformed replies
  210|       |    /// are reported as `subscribe/outcome_unknown` without another attempt.
  211|       |    /// After a sent command has an unknown result, a retry connection failure
  212|       |    /// or rejection also remains `subscribe/outcome_unknown`. The single
  213|       |    /// retry can succeed on `+OK`, or on `BUSYGROUP` when the request accepts
  214|       |    /// the existing cursor (`New` or explicit `resume`).

  565|      1|        block_on(async {
  566|      1|            let settings = RedisEventBusConfig::default();
  567|      1|            let capacity = settings.max_concurrent_commands() - settings.reserved_settlement_commands();
  568|      1|            let client = Arc::new(Client::new(&settings).expect("client"));
  569|      1|            let permits: Vec<_> = (0..capacity)
  570|     56|                .map(|_| {
                               ^1
  571|     56|                    client
  572|     56|                        .try_command(crate::client::CommandClass::General)
  573|     56|                        .expect("fill general admission")
  574|     56|                })
  575|      1|                .collect();
  576|      1|            crate::wire_fields::reset_encode_count();
  577|      1|            let bus = AsyncRedisEventBus {
  578|      1|                client: Arc::clone(&client),
  579|      1|                settings,
  580|      1|            };
  581|       |
  582|      1|            let result = bus.publish(outbound(vec![1; 1024])).await;
  583|       |
  584|      1|            assert_publish_error(
  585|      1|                result.expect_err("full admission must reject publish"),
  586|      1|                "resource_limit",
  587|       |            );
  588|      1|            assert_eq!(crate::wire_fields::encode_count(), 0);
  589|      1|            drop(permits);
  590|      1|            let permits: Vec<_> = (0..capacity)
  591|     56|                .map(|_| {
                               ^1
  592|     56|                    client
  593|     56|                        .try_command(crate::client::CommandClass::General)
  594|     56|                        .expect("admission was released")
  595|     56|                })
  596|      1|                .collect();
  597|      1|            assert_eq!(permits.len(), capacity);
  598|      1|        });
  599|      1|    }

  795|       |    #[test]
  796|      1|    fn test_publish_holds_one_general_permit_during_concurrent_encoding() {
  797|      1|        let _gate_lock = crate::wire_fields::encode_gate_test_lock();
  798|      1|        let settings = one_general_slot_settings();
  799|      1|        let client = Arc::new(Client::new(&settings).expect("client"));
  800|      1|        let bus = Arc::new(AsyncRedisEventBus {
  801|      1|            client: Arc::clone(&client),
  802|      1|            settings,
  803|      1|        });
  804|      1|        let (entered_tx, entered_rx) = std::sync::mpsc::channel();
  805|      1|        let (release_tx, release_rx) = std::sync::mpsc::channel();
  806|      1|        crate::wire_fields::install_encode_gate("test-encoding-gate", entered_tx, release_rx);
  807|      1|        let first_bus = Arc::clone(&bus);
  808|      1|        let first = thread::spawn(move || {
  809|      1|            crate::wire_fields::reset_encode_count();
  810|      1|            let result = block_on(first_bus.publish(gated_outbound()));
  811|      1|            (result, crate::wire_fields::encode_count())
  812|      1|        });
  813|       |
  814|      1|        if entered_rx.recv_timeout(Duration::from_secs(5)).is_err() {
  815|      0|            let _ = release_tx.send(());
  816|      0|            crate::wire_fields::clear_encode_gate();
  817|      0|            let _ = first.join();
  818|      0|            panic!("publisher did not reach the encoding gate");
  819|      1|        }
  820|      1|        let second_bus = Arc::clone(&bus);
  821|      1|        let second = thread::spawn(move || {
  822|      1|            crate::wire_fields::reset_encode_count();
  823|      1|            let result = block_on(second_bus.publish(gated_outbound()));
  824|      1|            (result, crate::wire_fields::encode_count())
  825|      1|        });
  826|      1|        let (second_result, second_encode_count) = second.join().expect("second publisher thread");
  827|      1|        let _ = release_tx.send(());
  828|      1|        let (first_result, first_encode_count) = first.join().expect("first publisher thread");
  829|      1|        crate::wire_fields::clear_encode_gate();
  830|      1|        assert_publish_error(
  831|      1|            second_result.expect_err("occupied encoding slot rejects another publish"),
  832|      1|            "resource_limit",
  833|       |        );
  834|      1|        assert_eq!(second_encode_count, 0);
  835|      1|        assert!(first_result.is_err(), "offline endpoint fails after encoding");
  836|      1|        assert_eq!(first_encode_count, 1);
  837|      1|        let permit = client
  838|      1|            .try_command(crate::client::CommandClass::General)
  839|      1|            .expect("first publisher releases its permit after setup failure");
  840|      1|        drop(permit);
  841|      1|    }

   31|       |/// Factory registered for runtime-neutral asynchronous Redis Streams access.
   32|       |///
   33|       |/// The provider creates Redis clients and async SPI objects without choosing or

   36|       |/// # Examples
   37|       |///
   38|       |/// ```

  139|       |    /// Reads pending deliveries before new group entries using a finite block.
  140|       |    ///
  141|       |    /// Normal calls before the next recovery deadline read new entries
  142|       |    /// directly. Cancellation leaves any Redis-delivered item in the pending
  143|       |    /// entries list and forces the next call to scan reclaimable and
  144|       |    /// own-pending entries before waiting for new group entries.
  145|       |    ///
  146|       |    /// # Parameters
  147|       |    ///
  148|       |    /// - `timeout`: Scheduling wait for new entries, zero for one non-blocking
  149|       |    ///   read per phase, or Duration::MAX for repeated finite BLOCK intervals.
  150|       |    ///   When local unsettled capacity is full, a positive timeout suspends for
  151|       |    ///   at most 10 ms (and no longer than `timeout`) before returning
  152|       |    ///   `TimedOut`; zero returns immediately. Runtime scheduling can make the
  153|       |    ///   actual delay longer. Socket/response waits are bounded separately and
  154|       |    ///   can extend past this deadline.

  421|       |    /// Acknowledges terminal decisions and leaves retry decisions in PEL.
  422|       |    ///
  423|       |    /// Accept and Reject atomically verify the PEL owner and issue `XACK`;
  424|       |    /// Retry only releases the receiver's local in-flight slot, leaving the
  425|       |    /// Redis entry available for redelivery. Applying the same disposition
  426|       |    /// again succeeds. Once settlement may have been sent, cancellation or
  427|       |    /// reply loss retains its intent and rejects conflicts.
  428|       |    ///
  429|       |    /// # Parameters

  812|       |///
  813|       |/// - `subscription`: Receiver supplying the topic, group, and recovery state.
  814|       |/// - `entry`: Stream entry returned by a read or claim command.

  823|       |/// is exceeded, `UnsupportedVersion` for a valid unknown wire version, or

  826|       |/// poison through its quarantine policy.

  833|    213|        &subscription.recovery,
  834|    213|        entry,
  835|    213|        subscription.wire_limits,
  836|       |    )
  837|    213|}

 1123|       |    /// Injects a private bookkeeping-lock fault after Redis acknowledges the
 1124|       |    /// real PEL entry. The adapter must return its safe local commit error
 1125|       |    /// and retain terminal intent.
 1126|       |    #[test]
 1127|      1|    fn test_settle_applied_xack_local_commit_failure_preserves_intent() -> Result<(), Box<dyn Error>> {
 1128|      1|        block_on(async {
 1129|      1|            let server = RedisServer::start()?;
                                                           ^0
 1130|      1|            let proxy = ControlledRedis::start(server.url())?;
                                                                          ^0
 1131|      1|            let mut subscription = subscription();

                                                                                                     ^0
 1133|      1|            subscription.client = Arc::new(Client::new(&settings)?);
                                                                               ^0
 1134|      1|            let mut observer = RedisClient::open(server.url())?.get_connection()?;
                                                                            ^0                ^0
 1135|      1|            cmd("XADD")

                                                             ^0

                                                         ^0

                                                   ^0

 1184|      1|                gate.wait_applied().await;

 1199|      1|                    *progress.lock().expect("progress healthy"),
 1200|       |                    SettlementProgress::AckPending(DeliveryDisposition::Accept)
 1201|       |                );
 1202|      1|                let poisoned = Arc::clone(&recovery);
 1203|       |                // Internal fault injection targets local bookkeeping after real Redis ACK.
 1204|      1|                assert!(
 1205|      1|                    spawn(move || {
 1206|      1|                        let _guard = poisoned.lock().expect("recovery initially healthy");
 1207|      1|                        panic!("inject poisoned recovery between applied settlement EVAL and local commit");
 1208|       |                    })

 1211|       |                );

 1214|      0|            })
 1215|      1|            .await;

 1217|      1|            let error = result.expect_err("applied settlement EVAL must not falsely report a successful local commit");
 1218|       |            let SpiError::Operation {
 1219|      1|                operation,
 1220|      1|                kind,
 1221|      1|                retryable,
 1222|      1|                source,
 1223|       |                ..
 1224|      1|            } = error
 1225|       |            else {
 1226|      0|                panic!("local commit fault must map to a sanitized operation error");
 1227|       |            };
 1228|      1|            assert_eq!(operation, "settle");
 1229|      1|            assert_eq!(kind, "redis_error");
 1230|      1|            assert_eq!(retryable, None);
 1231|      1|            assert!(matches!(
 1232|      1|                source.downcast_ref::<RedisProviderError>(),
 1233|      1|                Some(RedisProviderError::Operation("recovery lock"))
 1234|       |            ));
 1235|      1|            assert_eq!(
 1236|      1|                *progress.lock().expect("progress remains healthy"),
 1237|       |                SettlementProgress::AckPending(DeliveryDisposition::Accept)
 1238|       |            );
 1239|      1|            assert_eq!(
 1240|      1|                recovery
 1241|      1|                    .lock()
 1242|      1|                    .err()
 1243|      1|                    .expect("recovery remains poisoned")
 1244|      1|                    .into_inner()
 1245|      1|                    .active_len(),
 1246|       |                1
 1247|       |            );
 1248|      2|            for conflict in [DeliveryDisposition::Retry, DeliveryDisposition::Reject] {
                                          ^1
 1249|      2|                assert!(matches!(
                                      ^0
 1250|      2|                    subscription.settle(&token, conflict).await,
 1251|       |                    Err(SpiError::InvalidSettlementToken {
 1252|       |                        retryable: Some(false),
 1253|       |                        ..
 1254|       |                    })
 1255|       |                ));
 1256|       |            }
 1257|      1|            recovery.clear_poison();
 1258|       |            // The PEL was cleared above: this identical retry obtains EVAL=0.
 1259|      1|            subscription.settle(&token, DeliveryDisposition::Accept).await?;
                                                                                        ^0
 1260|      1|            assert_eq!(
 1261|      1|                *progress.lock().expect("progress healthy"),
 1262|       |                SettlementProgress::Applied(DeliveryDisposition::Accept)
 1263|       |            );
 1264|      1|            assert_eq!(recovery.lock().expect("recovery repaired").active_len(), 0);
 1265|      1|            subscription.settle(&token, DeliveryDisposition::Accept).await?;
                                                                                        ^0
 1266|      1|            Ok(())
 1267|      1|        })

  742|       |    /// Local RESP endpoint recording setup and pipeline traffic; modes inject
  743|       |    /// non-PONG, ECHO disconnect, and PING disconnect without changing
  744|       |    /// production.
  745|       |    #[cfg(feature = "sync")]
  746|       |    struct LeaseEndpoint {
  747|       |        url: String,
  748|       |        stop: Arc<AtomicBool>,
  749|       |        mode: Arc<AtomicUsize>,
  750|       |        accepted: Arc<AtomicUsize>,
  751|       |        commands: Arc<Mutex<Vec<Vec<String>>>>,
  752|       |        sockets: Arc<Mutex<Vec<TcpStream>>>,
  753|       |        worker: Option<JoinHandle<()>>,
  754|       |    }
  755|       |
  756|       |    #[cfg(feature = "sync")]
  757|       |    impl LeaseEndpoint {
  758|       |        /// Starts a local RESP endpoint recording lease health and pipeline
  759|       |        /// traffic.
  760|       |        ///
  761|       |        /// # Returns
  762|       |        ///
  763|       |        /// An owned endpoint with TCP workers that are joined on drop. Binding,
  764|       |        /// accepting, and serving requests perform blocking socket I/O.
  765|       |        ///
  766|       |        /// # Panics
  767|       |        ///
  768|       |        /// Panics if fixture socket setup or thread creation fails; workers
  769|       |        /// also panic on malformed or unexpected fixture commands.
  770|      2|        fn new() -> Self {
  771|      2|            let listener = TcpListener::bind("127.0.0.1:0").expect("lease endpoint");
  772|      2|            let url = format!("redis://{}/3", listener.local_addr().expect("address"));
  773|      2|            listener.set_nonblocking(true).expect("nonblocking listener");
  774|      2|            let stop = Arc::new(AtomicBool::new(false));
  775|      2|            let mode = Arc::new(AtomicUsize::new(0));
  776|      2|            let accepted = Arc::new(AtomicUsize::new(0));
  777|      2|            let commands = Arc::new(Mutex::new(Vec::new()));

  900|       |    /// argument header, before a complete request was read.

                                                    ^0

  922|     26|        for _ in 0..count {

   18|       |    /// is dropped.

   24|       |    /// Releases exactly the slot acquired for this application operation.

   32|  5.19k|            CommandLane::General => self.budget.commands.fetch_sub(1, Ordering::AcqRel),

    8|       |//! Bounded synchronous connection reuse.

   18|       |

   20|       |use super::sync_connection_pool::SyncConnectionPool;
   21|       |
   22|       |/// Owns one blocking socket and optionally returns healthy short-command
   23|       |/// leases.
   24|       |///
   25|       |/// Dedicated receiver sockets have no idle pool or short-command permit here;
   26|       |/// their receiver admission is retained by the subscription itself.
   27|       |pub(crate) struct PooledConnection {
   28|       |    /// Socket remains present until Drop takes ownership for idle retention.
   29|       |    connection: Option<Connection>,
   30|       |    /// `Some` allows healthy idle retention; `None` forces socket destruction.
   31|       |    pool: Option<Arc<SyncConnectionPool>>,
   32|       |    /// Short-command admission, or `None` for a dedicated receiver connection.
   33|       |    _permit: Option<CommandPermit>,
   34|       |}
   35|       |
   36|       |#[cfg(feature = "sync")]
   37|       |impl PooledConnection {
   38|       |    /// Wraps an already initialized socket without performing network I/O.
   39|       |    ///
   40|       |    /// # Parameters
   41|       |    ///
   42|       |    /// - `connection`: Socket with controlled setup and command waits.
   43|       |    /// - `pool`: Idle-retention destination, or `None` for a dedicated socket.
   44|       |    /// - `permit`: Acquired short-command slot, or `None` for a receiver
   45|       |    ///   socket.
   46|       |    ///
   47|       |    /// # Returns
   48|       |    ///
   49|       |    /// A lease that releases admission and disposes or retains the socket on
   50|       |    /// drop.
   51|       |    #[must_use]
   52|       |    #[inline]
   53|  3.49k|    pub(crate) fn new(
   54|  3.49k|        connection: Connection,
   55|  3.49k|        pool: Option<Arc<SyncConnectionPool>>,
   56|  3.49k|        permit: Option<CommandPermit>,
   57|  3.49k|    ) -> Self {
   58|  3.49k|        Self {
   59|  3.49k|            connection: Some(connection),
   60|  3.49k|            pool,
   61|  3.49k|            _permit: permit,
   62|  3.49k|        }
   63|  3.49k|    }
   64|       |
   65|       |    /// Sets the socket read wait to `timeout`; `None` clears that wait.

  741|       |        use futures_lite::future::block_on;
  742|       |

  747|       |        ])
  748|      1|        .expect("scripted Sentinel");
  749|      1|        let config = RedisEventBusConfig::from_provider_options(&resolver_settings(&sentinel, "master"))
  750|      1|            .expect("valid settings");

  752|      1|        let cache = &resolver.async_control_connections[0];
  753|       |

  756|       |
  757|      1|        let unpolled_query = resolver.query_target_async(0);
  758|      1|        drop(unpolled_query);
  759|      1|        assert_eq!(
  760|      1|            cache.published_generation(),
  761|       |            Some(1),
  762|       |            "unpolled query leaves lease intact"
  763|       |        );
  764|       |
  765|      1|        drop(SentinelQueryGuard::new(cache, 1));
  766|      1|        assert_eq!(
  767|      1|            cache.published_generation(),
  768|       |            Some(1),
  769|       |            "unstarted query leaves lease intact"
  770|       |        );
  771|       |
  772|      1|        let mut stale_guard = SentinelQueryGuard::new(cache, 1);
  773|      1|        stale_guard.mark_started();
  774|      1|        let mut canceled_guard = SentinelQueryGuard::new(cache, 1);
  775|      1|        canceled_guard.mark_started();
  776|      1|        drop(canceled_guard);
  777|      1|        assert_eq!(
  778|      1|            cache.published_generation(),
  779|       |            None,
  780|       |            "started abandoned query invalidates its lease"
  781|       |        );
  782|       |
  783|      1|        drop(block_on(resolver.connect_async(None)).expect("replacement master connection"));
  784|      1|        assert_eq!(cache.published_generation(), Some(2));
  785|      1|        drop(stale_guard);
  786|      1|        assert_eq!(
  787|      1|            cache.published_generation(),
  788|       |            Some(2),
  789|       |            "stale guard cannot invalidate replacement"
  790|       |        );
  791|       |
  792|      1|        sentinel.finish();
  793|      1|        master.finish();
  794|      1|        assert_eq!(sentinel.connection_count(), 2);
  795|      1|        assert_eq!(master.connection_count(), 2);
  796|      1|    }

   16|       |/// Stores only idle synchronous short-command connections.
   17|       |///

   19|       |/// admission budget; dedicated receiver sockets never enter this pool.

   24|       |    /// Maximum number of idle connections retained.

   26|       |}

   32|       |    /// The directory lock covers only weak-reference upgrades and removal of

   34|       |    /// provider feature enabled, this returns an empty vector.

   49|       |/// Enforces the wire-byte limit, validates the complete JSON value and reads
   50|       |/// its version, then constructs bounded version-one fields. Serde's JSON
   51|       |/// recursion guard applies during the first pass for every version, including
   52|       |/// ignored future fields. A valid unknown version is preserved without
   53|       |/// interpreting its version-one layout. These parser limits cover the decoded
   54|       |/// wire record, payload, and headers; they do not cap the client's RESP frame
   55|       |/// allocation. The dedicated receiver applies a separate limit to the complete
   56|       |/// RESP response frame.
   57|       |///
   58|       |/// # Parameters
   59|       |///
   60|       |/// - `encoded`: Serialized wire record to validate and decode.

   65|       |/// The validated version-one wire fields.
   66|       |///
   67|       |/// # Errors
   68|       |///
   69|       |/// Returns a sanitized malformed JSON, unsupported version, or limit error.
   70|       |/// Invalid or excessively nested JSON is malformed even when its version is
   71|       |/// unknown; only a fully valid document with an unsupported version is

    8|       |//! Settlement intent retained across unknown acknowledgement outcomes.
    9|       |
   10|       |use qubit_event_bus::spi::DeliveryDisposition;
   11|       |
   12|       |use super::settlement_action::SettlementAction;
   13|       |
   14|       |/// Local settlement progress for one receiver-bound token.
   15|       |#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Eq, PartialEq)]
   16|       |pub(crate) enum SettlementProgress {
   17|       |    /// No acknowledgement may have applied and no disposition is committed.
   18|       |    Open,
   19|       |    /// A terminal acknowledgement may have applied; only its intent may repeat.
   20|       |    AckPending(
   21|       |        /// Terminal intent whose prior XACK may have applied; only this intent
   22|       |        /// may repeat.
   23|       |        DeliveryDisposition,
   24|       |    ),
   25|       |    /// Local bookkeeping committed this disposition; terminal outcomes also
   26|       |    /// have a valid XACK reply, while Retry commits without Redis I/O.
   27|       |    Applied(
   28|       |        /// Disposition already committed to local active-slot bookkeeping.
   29|       |        DeliveryDisposition,
   30|       |    ),
   31|       |}
   32|       |
   33|       |impl SettlementProgress {
   34|       |    /// Selects the next operation without changing progress or issuing I/O.
   35|       |    ///
   36|       |    /// # Parameters
   37|       |    ///
   38|       |    /// - `requested`: The disposition requested for this token.
   39|       |    ///
   40|       |    /// # Returns
   41|       |    ///
   42|       |    /// The acknowledgement preparation, identical acknowledgement retry,
   43|       |    /// local Retry commit, or already-applied action permitted by this state.
   44|       |    ///
   45|       |    /// # Errors
   46|       |    ///
   47|       |    /// Returns `Err(())` when `requested` conflicts with a pending or applied
   48|       |    /// disposition. Connection acquisition must precede fixing a new intent.
   49|       |    #[inline]
   50|  1.30k|    pub(crate) fn action(self, requested: DeliveryDisposition) -> Result<SettlementAction, ()> {
   51|  1.18k|        match self {
   52|     80|            Self::Applied(previous) if previous == requested => Ok(SettlementAction::AlreadyApplied),
                                        ^36                              ^36  ^36
   53|     45|            Self::AckPending(previous) if previous == requested => Ok(SettlementAction::RepeatAck),
                                           ^21                              ^21  ^21
   54|     68|            Self::Applied(_) | Self::AckPending(_) => Err(()),
   55|  1.18k|            Self::Open if requested == DeliveryDisposition::Retry => Ok(SettlementAction::ApplyRetry),
                                                                              ^38  ^38
   56|  1.14k|            Self::Open => Ok(SettlementAction::PrepareAck),
   57|       |        }
   58|  1.30k|    }

    8|       |//! Shared Redis settlement metadata retained by synchronous and asynchronous
    9|       |//! tokens.
   10|       |
   11|       |use std::sync::Arc;
   12|       |use std::sync::Mutex;
   13|       |
   14|       |use qubit_event_bus::spi::DeliveryDisposition;
   15|       |
   16|       |use super::recovery_state::RecoveryState;
   17|       |use super::settlement_progress::SettlementProgress;
   18|       |
   19|       |/// Redis coordinates and shared settlement bookkeeping carried by one token.
   20|       |#[derive(Clone)]
   21|       |pub(crate) struct SettlementState {
   22|       |    /// Stream containing the pending event.
   23|       |    pub(crate) stream: String,
   24|       |    /// Consumer group that owns the pending entry.
   25|       |    pub(crate) group: String,
   26|       |    /// Redis stream ID used by `XACK`.
   27|       |    pub(crate) message_id: String,
   28|       |    /// Fixed acknowledgement intent and locally committed disposition.
   29|       |    pub(crate) progress: Arc<Mutex<SettlementProgress>>,
   30|       |    /// Per-subscription active delivery registry released after settlement.
   31|       |    pub(crate) recovery: Arc<Mutex<RecoveryState>>,
   32|       |}
   33|       |
   34|       |impl SettlementState {
   35|       |    /// Commits `disposition` and releases its active slot without I/O.
   36|       |    ///
   37|       |    /// Locks progress before recovery, acquiring both before mutating either.
   38|       |    /// The caller must have validated this disposition and, for terminal
   39|       |    /// outcomes, received a valid XACK reply. Neither lock spans an await.
   40|       |    ///
   41|       |    /// # Parameters
   42|       |    ///
   43|       |    /// - `disposition`: The permitted disposition to commit locally.
   44|       |    ///
   45|       |    /// # Returns
   46|       |    ///
   47|       |    /// Success once the active slot is released and progress becomes Applied.
   48|       |    ///
   49|       |    /// # Errors
   50|       |    ///
   51|       |    /// Returns a stable lock label when poisoning prevents the full commit.
   52|       |    /// Neither state changes; pending acknowledgement intent remains fixed
   53|       |    /// so only the identical terminal decision may retry.
   54|  1.15k|    pub(crate) fn commit(&self, disposition: DeliveryDisposition) -> Result<(), &'static str> {
   55|  1.15k|        let mut progress = self.progress.lock().map_err(|_| "settlement lock")?;
                          ^1.15k                                                            ^1
   56|  1.15k|        let mut recovery = self.recovery.lock().map_err(|_| "recovery lock")?;
                          ^1.14k                                                          ^3
   57|  1.14k|        if disposition == DeliveryDisposition::Retry {
   58|     37|            recovery.mark_retry(&self.message_id);
   59|  1.11k|        } else {
   60|  1.11k|            recovery.mark_terminal(&self.message_id);
   61|  1.11k|        }
   62|  1.14k|        *progress = SettlementProgress::Applied(disposition);
   63|  1.14k|        Ok(())
   64|  1.15k|    }
   65|       |}

    8|       |//! Collision-safe Redis key construction.
    9|       |
   10|       |/// Builds a Redis stream key whose length-prefixed components cannot collide.
   11|       |///
   12|       |/// The namespace and topic are encoded as UTF-8 byte lengths followed by their
   13|       |/// original contents. This keeps component boundaries unambiguous even when a
   14|       |/// value contains `:`. The function does not validate either input; callers
   15|       |/// that accept user configuration should validate it before key construction.
   16|       |///
   17|       |/// # Parameters
   18|       |///
   19|       |/// - `namespace`: Application scope shared by related providers.
   20|       |/// - `topic`: Logical topic whose events are stored in the stream.
   21|       |///
   22|       |/// # Returns
   23|       |///
   24|       |/// A stable Redis key for this namespace and topic pair.
   25|       |///
   26|       |/// # Examples
   27|       |///
   28|       |/// ```
   29|       |/// use qubit_event_bus_redis::naming::stream_key;
   30|       |///
   31|       |/// let key = stream_key("orders", "created");
   32|       |/// assert_eq!(key, "qubit:stream:6:orders:7:created");
   33|       |/// ```
   34|       |#[must_use]
   35|  4.53k|pub fn stream_key(namespace: &str, topic: &str) -> String {
   36|  4.53k|    format!("qubit:stream:{}:{namespace}:{}:{topic}", namespace.len(), topic.len())
   37|  4.53k|}
   38|       |
   39|       |/// Builds a stable Redis consumer-group name from subscription identity.
   40|       |///
   41|       |/// When `group` is `None`, the subscriber ID becomes the group identity. An
   42|       |/// explicit group lets several subscribers share work while a different group
   43|       |/// receives its own copy of every stream entry. Component lengths count UTF-8
   44|       |/// bytes, so values containing `:` remain unambiguous.
   45|       |///
   46|       |/// # Parameters
   47|       |///
   48|       |/// - `namespace`: Application scope used by the corresponding stream key.
   49|       |/// - `topic`: Logical topic consumed by the group.
   50|       |/// - `subscriber`: Stable subscriber identity used when no group is supplied.
   51|       |/// - `group`: Optional shared group identity.
   52|       |///
   53|       |/// # Returns
   54|       |///
   55|       |/// A deterministic Redis consumer-group name.
   56|       |///
   57|       |/// # Examples
   58|       |///
   59|       |/// ```
   60|       |/// use qubit_event_bus_redis::naming::group_name;
   61|       |///
   62|       |/// assert_eq!(
   63|       |///     group_name("orders", "created", "billing", None),
   64|       |///     group_name("orders", "created", "billing", Some("billing")),
   65|       |/// );
   66|       |/// ```

    9|       |
   10|       |#[cfg(feature = "sync")]
   11|       |use redis::ConnectionLike;
   12|       |use redis::RedisError;

   22|       |/// Lua preflights types and ownership, copies the last wire field, then ACKs.
   23|       |const QUARANTINE_SCRIPT: &str = r#"
   24|       |local source_type = redis.call('TYPE', KEYS[1]).ok
   25|       |if source_type == 'none' then return 0 end
   26|       |if source_type ~= 'stream' then return redis.error_reply('WRONGTYPE source must be a stream') end
   27|       |local quarantine_type = redis.call('TYPE', KEYS[2]).ok
   28|       |if quarantine_type ~= 'none' and quarantine_type ~= 'stream' then
   29|       |    return redis.error_reply('WRONGTYPE quarantine must be a stream')
   30|       |end
   31|       |local pending = redis.call('XPENDING', KEYS[1], ARGV[1], ARGV[3], ARGV[3], 1)
   32|       |if #pending == 0 then return 0 end
   33|       |if pending[1][2] ~= ARGV[2] then return -1 end
   34|       |local rows = redis.call('XRANGE', KEYS[1], ARGV[3], ARGV[3])
   35|       |if #rows == 0 then
   36|       |    local acknowledged = redis.call('XACK', KEYS[1], ARGV[1], ARGV[3])
   37|       |    if acknowledged == 1 then return 2 end
   38|       |    return 0
   39|       |end
   40|       |local fields = rows[1][2]
   41|       |local wire = ''
   42|       |local missing = '1'
   43|       |for index = 1, #fields, 2 do
   44|       |    if fields[index] == 'wire' then
   45|       |        wire = fields[index + 1]
   46|       |        missing = '0'
   47|       |    end
   48|       |end
   49|       |redis.call('XADD', KEYS[2], '*',
   50|       |    'source_stream', KEYS[1],
   51|       |    'source_id', ARGV[3],
   52|       |    'group', ARGV[1],
   53|       |    'reason', ARGV[4],
   54|       |    'wire', wire,
   55|       |    'wire_missing', missing)
   56|       |local acknowledged = redis.call('XACK', KEYS[1], ARGV[1], ARGV[3])
   57|       |if acknowledged ~= 1 then return -2 end
   58|       |return 1
   59|       |"#;

   61|       |/// Transfers a malformed pending entry without interleaving other commands.
   62|       |///
   63|       |/// # Type Parameters
   64|       |///
   65|       |/// - `C`: Controlled blocking transport implementing Redis command execution.
   66|       |///
   67|       |/// # Parameters
   68|       |///
   69|       |/// - `connection`: Redis connection used for the single Lua command.
   70|       |/// - `source`: Stream key from which the malformed entry was read.
   71|       |/// - `quarantine`: Per-group quarantine stream key.
   72|       |/// - `group`: Consumer group whose pending entry is being settled.

   76|       |///

   80|       |///

   84|       |/// status. Type and owner checks precede writes, but Redis Lua does not roll
   85|       |/// back an `XADD` when a subsequent `XACK` fails. A lost reply can hide a
   86|       |/// successful copy and acknowledgement; callers must preserve that uncertainty
   87|       |/// and inspect the source PEL before any later recovery attempt.

   96|     33|    reason: PoisonReason,

   98|     33|    let result: Result<Value, RedisError> = cmd("EVAL")
   99|     33|        .arg(QUARANTINE_SCRIPT)
  100|     33|        .arg(2)
  101|     33|        .arg(source)
  102|     33|        .arg(quarantine)
  103|     33|        .arg(group)

  105|     33|        .arg(id)

   28|       |/// Validated connection, namespace, recovery, and in-flight limits for Redis.
   29|       |///
   30|       |/// Configuration parsed from provider options rejects inline URL credentials
   31|       |/// and resolves credential environment-variable references immediately. Its
   32|       |/// `Debug` output redacts both the URL and credential values.
   33|       |///
   34|       |/// # Examples
   35|       |///
   36|       |/// ```
   37|       |/// use qubit_event_bus_redis::config::RedisEventBusConfig;
   38|       |///
   39|       |/// let config = RedisEventBusConfig::new("redis://127.0.0.1/", "orders").expect("valid config");
   40|       |/// assert_eq!(config.namespace(), "orders");
   41|       |/// assert_eq!(config.connection_url(), "redis://127.0.0.1/");
   42|       |/// ```
   43|       |#[derive(Clone)]
   44|       |pub struct RedisEventBusConfig {
   45|       |    /// Redis connection URL without embedded credentials.
   46|       |    connection_url: String,
   47|       |    /// Application namespace used when deriving stream and group keys.

    8|       |//! Explicit durable subscription policy for Redis Streams consumers.
    9|       |
   10|       |use qubit_event_bus::model::ConsumerGroup;
   11|       |use qubit_event_bus::model::StartPosition;
   12|       |use qubit_event_bus::model::SubscribeOptions;
   13|       |use qubit_event_bus::model::SubscribeOptionsBuilder;
   14|       |use qubit_event_bus::model::SubscriptionDurability;
   15|       |
   16|       |/// Redis subscription settings shared across typed subscriber requests.
   17|       |///
   18|       |/// A start position is required for each profile. Redis subscriptions are
   19|       |/// durable; the returned builder can add ACK, retry, and middleware policies.
   20|       |/// Changing its durability to `Ephemeral` is rejected by the Redis provider's
   21|       |/// capability check when subscribing.
   22|       |///
   23|       |/// ```
   24|       |/// use qubit_event_bus::model::ConsumerGroup;
   25|       |/// use qubit_event_bus::model::StartPosition;
   26|       |/// use qubit_event_bus::model::SubscriptionDurability;
   27|       |/// use qubit_event_bus_redis::RedisSubscriptionProfile;
   28|       |///
   29|       |/// let group = ConsumerGroup::new("billing")?;
   30|       |/// let options = RedisSubscriptionProfile::new(StartPosition::Earliest)
   31|       |///     .consumer_group(group.clone())
   32|       |///     .options::<String>()
   33|       |///     .build();
   34|       |/// assert_eq!(options.durability(), SubscriptionDurability::Durable);
   35|       |/// assert_eq!(options.start_position(), &StartPosition::Earliest);
   36|       |/// assert_eq!(options.consumer_group(), Some(&group));
   37|       |/// # Ok::<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>>(())
   38|       |/// ```
   39|       |#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
   40|       |#[must_use]
   41|       |pub struct RedisSubscriptionProfile {
   42|       |    /// Required start position copied into each options builder.
   43|       |    start_position: StartPosition,

   45|       |    consumer_group: Option<ConsumerGroup>,
   46|       |}

   28|       |/// Factory registered for synchronous Redis Streams access.
   29|       |///
   30|       |/// It validates provider options and creates the SPI implementation without
   31|       |/// opening a network connection. Short commands acquire admission-owning
   32|       |/// leases and reuse the bounded standalone idle pool; each receiver owns a
   33|       |/// dedicated connection until close or drop. Sentinel leases resolve the master
   34|       |/// afresh.
   35|       |///
   36|       |/// # Examples
   37|       |///
   38|       |/// ```
   39|       |/// use qubit_event_bus::registry::EventBusConfig;
   40|       |/// use qubit_event_bus::spi::EventBusSpi;
   41|       |/// use qubit_event_bus_redis::sync::RedisEventBusProvider;
   42|       |/// use qubit_spi::ServiceProvider;
   43|       |///
   44|       |/// let config = EventBusConfig::default();
   45|       |/// let bus = RedisEventBusProvider
   46|       |///     .create_configured(&config)
   47|       |///     .expect("default Redis settings are valid");
   48|       |/// let _capabilities = bus.capabilities();
   49|       |/// ```

  138|       |impl EventSubscriptionSpi for Subscription {
  139|       |    /// Recovers pending work without redelivering locally active entries.
  140|       |    ///
  141|       |    /// The first call and later calls after the recovery interval scan
  142|       |    /// reclaimable and own-pending entries before new group entries. Calls
  143|       |    /// before that deadline read new entries directly. Pending IDs already
  144|       |    /// held by local handlers are skipped. Malformed entries are copied to the
  145|       |    /// group quarantine stream and acknowledged by one serialized Lua
  146|       |    /// invocation. Lua does not roll back prior writes on failure, and a lost
  147|       |    /// reply leaves the transfer outcome unknown. Closing the receiver does
  148|       |    /// not acknowledge valid work.
  149|       |    ///
  150|       |    /// # Parameters
  151|       |    ///
  152|       |    /// - `timeout`: Scheduling wait for new entries, zero for bounded
  153|       |    ///   non-blocking phase reads, or Duration::MAX for repeated finite BLOCK
  154|       |    ///   intervals. When local unsettled capacity is full, a positive timeout
  155|       |    ///   sleeps for at most 10 ms (and no longer than `timeout`) before
  156|       |    ///   returning `TimedOut`; zero returns immediately. OS scheduling can make
  157|       |    ///   the actual delay longer. Individual I/O waits can also extend beyond
  158|       |    ///   the receive scheduling deadline.
  159|       |    ///
  160|       |    /// # Returns
  161|       |    ///
  162|       |    /// A message, a gap for removed or quarantined entries, a timeout, or
  163|       |    /// `Closed`.
  164|       |    ///
  165|       |    /// # Errors
  166|       |    ///
  167|       |    /// Returns a classified SPI error for admission, locks, connection/setup,
  168|       |    /// command/protocol failure, or unsupported wire version. Unknown read
  169|       |    /// outcomes recover through later PEL scans; an uncertain quarantine is

  432|       |    /// Acknowledges terminal dispositions and leaves retries in the PEL.
  433|       |    ///
  434|       |    /// Accept and Reject atomically verify the PEL owner and issue `XACK`;
  435|       |    /// Retry only releases the receiver's local in-flight slot and leaves
  436|       |    /// the Redis entry pending. Repeating the same disposition succeeds.
  437|       |    /// Once settlement may have been sent, its intent remains fixed across
  438|       |    /// reply loss; conflicting dispositions are rejected.
  439|       |    ///
  440|       |    /// # Parameters
  441|       |    ///
  442|       |    /// - `token`: Settlement token produced by this receiver.
  443|       |    /// - `disposition`: Terminal action or retry decision to apply.
  444|       |    ///
  445|       |    /// # Returns

  448|       |    /// Terminal decisions require a valid settlement reply (0 or 1); Retry
  449|       |    /// commits locally without acknowledging Redis. Repeating the applied
  450|       |    /// intent is idempotent, while unknown prior ACK intent remains fixed.
  451|       |    ///
  452|       |    /// # Errors

  586|       |/// Decodes a record or transfers a deterministic decode failure to quarantine.

   31|       |/// Clears deleted pending records while preserving the scan cursor and budget.
   32|       |///
   33|       |/// The claim reply's deleted-ID count is included in the result. Additional
   34|       |/// stale IDs are probed through bounded `XPENDING` and `XRANGE` reads, then
   35|       |/// cleared through the existing atomic quarantine script. A partial scan
   36|       |/// retains its cursor so a later receive can resume it.
   37|       |///
   38|       |/// # Parameters
   39|       |///
   40|       |/// - `subscription`: Receiver whose Redis keys, cursor state, and idle policy
   41|       |///   govern the scan.
   42|       |/// - `connection`: Dedicated receive socket used for Redis commands.
   43|       |/// - `driver`: Per-receive command and maintenance budget.
   44|       |/// - `has_missing_entries`: Whether Redis reported deleted IDs in XAUTOCLAIM.
   45|       |/// - `deleted_count`: Number of deleted IDs already reported by XAUTOCLAIM.
   46|       |///
   47|       |/// # Returns
   48|       |///
   49|       |/// The combined number of deleted pending records found in this scan.
   50|       |///
   51|       |/// # Errors
   52|       |///
   53|       |/// Returns classified receiver-operation or socket errors, or an
   54|       |/// outcome-unknown error when a Redis reply or quarantine result cannot be
   55|       |/// trusted.
   56|    334|pub(super) fn scan(

                                             ^12^12

  149|      2|        lock_state(&subscription.recovery, &subscription.topic, "receive", "recovery lock")?.set_tombstone_cursor(id);
                                                                                                         ^0
  150|       |    }

  131|       |/// Versioned event fields stored in one Redis Stream record.
  132|       |///
  133|       |/// This is the concrete Rust layout for version 1 Redis stream fields. The
  134|       |/// payload is a byte vector and headers are represented as JSON. Its derived
  135|       |/// [`Deserialize`] implementation requires this layout directly; it does not
  136|       |/// perform the provider's version-first classification or enforce configured
  137|       |/// wire, payload, or header size limits. The provider's internal decoder first
  138|       |/// validates JSON and reads the version, then applies bounded version 1 field
  139|       |/// decoding. A complete version 1-shaped value with an unknown version can be
  140|       |/// deserialized as this type, but [`WireFields::into_parts`] rejects it. An
  141|       |/// arbitrary future layout need not deserialize as `WireFields` at all.
  142|       |///
  143|       |/// # Examples
  144|       |///
  145|       |/// ```
  146|       |/// use std::sync::Arc;
  147|       |/// use std::time::SystemTime;
  148|       |///
  149|       |/// use qubit_event_bus::model::ContentType;
  150|       |/// use qubit_event_bus::model::EventId;
  151|       |/// use qubit_event_bus::model::Headers;
  152|       |/// use qubit_event_bus::spi::EncodedPayload;
  153|       |/// use qubit_event_bus::spi::OutboundMessage;
  154|       |/// use qubit_event_bus::spi::TopicAddress;
  155|       |/// use qubit_event_bus::spi::TransportPayload;
  156|       |/// use qubit_event_bus_redis::wire::WireFields;
  157|       |///
  158|       |/// # fn main() -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
  159|       |/// let message = OutboundMessage::new(
  160|       |///     TopicAddress::new("orders.created")?,
  161|       |///     EventId::new("evt-42")?,
  162|       |///     SystemTime::UNIX_EPOCH,
  163|       |///     Headers::new(),
  164|       |///     None,
  165|       |///     None,
  166|       |///     TransportPayload::Encoded(EncodedPayload::new(
  167|       |///         Arc::from(&b"order-42"[..]),
  168|       |///         ContentType::new("text/plain")?,
  169|       |///         None,
  170|       |///     )),
  171|       |/// );
  172|       |/// let fields = WireFields::from_outbound(&message)?;
  173|       |/// assert_eq!(fields.version, 1);
  174|       |/// assert_eq!(fields.payload, b"order-42");
  175|       |/// # Ok(())
  176|       |/// # }
  177|       |/// ```
  178|       |#[derive(Clone, Debug, Deserialize, Serialize)]
  179|       |pub struct WireFields {
  180|       |    /// Protocol version used to select the field decoder.
  181|       |    pub version: u32,
  182|       |    /// Stable event identifier preserved across transport boundaries.
  183|       |    pub event_id: String,
  184|       |    /// Event timestamp as whole milliseconds since the Unix epoch.
  185|       |    pub timestamp_ms: u128,
  186|       |    /// Serialized string-valued event headers.
  187|       |    pub headers_json: String,
  188|       |    /// Optional ordering metadata; Redis does not enforce ordering by this key.
  189|       |    pub ordering_key: Option<String>,
  190|       |    /// MIME-like content type used by the facade codec registry.
  191|       |    pub content_type: String,
  192|       |    /// Optional schema identifier supplied by the encoder.
  193|       |    pub schema_id: Option<String>,
  194|       |    /// Encoded payload bytes, serialized as JSON integer values.
  195|       |    pub payload: Vec<u8>,
  196|       |}
  197|       |
